⏺ I made a favicon and opened a PR for it:
  https://github.com/amahaja5/lit-graph/pull/7

  The design: a white "G" on a Zotero-red rounded square, matching the red "G"
  in the header. The G is drawn as a shape rather than typed in a font, so it
  looks the same on every computer. I checked it at 16px and 32px, the sizes
  browser tabs use, and it's still readable.

  Files added (in frontend/, so they're served from the site root):
  - favicon.svg: the main icon for modern browsers.
  - favicon.ico: 16, 32 and 48px versions in one file, for browsers and tools
    that ask for /favicon.ico automatically.
  - apple-touch-icon.png (180px): for adding the site to an iPhone or iPad home
    screen. It's a full square, because iOS rounds the corners itself.
  - A red theme-color tag, so mobile browsers tint their address bar to match.

  Checked: the local server returns all three files as images, not the app page.
  The one thing left is to confirm the tab icon on the PR's preview deployment,
  and that Vercel serves /favicon.ico directly rather than sending the app page
  in its place.

✻ Sautéed for 2m 0s · done 7:32 PM

❯ do i need to deploy on vercel?                                                

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ No, there's nothing to deploy by hand. Your Vercel project is connected to the
  GitHub repo, so:

  - Each PR gets a preview deployment automatically. PR #7's preview has already
    built successfully. Its link is on the PR page, under the Vercel check or
    in Vercel's comment.
  - Merging into main deploys to production automatically. When you merge PR #7,
    the favicon goes live on your domain about a minute later.

  You'd only need to redeploy by hand in Vercel after changing environment 
  variables there. Settings are read when the site is built, so code merges pick
  them up but a settings change on its own doesn't. That doesn't apply here.

  One tip: browsers cache favicons aggressively. If the tab still shows the old
  blank icon after merging, hard-refresh the page or open it in a private
  window.

✻ Cogitated for 8s · done 7:34 PM

❯ how do i change the url on vercel?                                            

⏺ Here's how to move from lit-graph.avijmahajan.com to a new address, for
  example groundwork.avijmahajan.com. This app needs a few extra steps beyond
  the usual Vercel ones, because Zotero sign-in is tied to the exact web
  address.

  1. Add the new domain in Vercel
  - Vercel dashboard → your project → Settings → Domains → Add, then enter
    groundwork.avijmahajan.com.
  - Vercel will show the DNS record to create. For a subdomain it's usually a
    CNAME from groundwork to cname.vercel-dns.com. Add it wherever
    avijmahajan.com's DNS is managed. If Vercel already manages that DNS, it's
    done for you.
  - Wait until the domain shows as valid, with its certificate issued.

  2. Point Zotero sign-in at the new address (otherwise sign-in breaks)
  - In Settings → Environment Variables, change ZOTERO_OAUTH_ORIGIN to
    https://groundwork.avijmahajan.com. Do this for every environment you sign
    in on.
  - At zotero.org/oauth/apps, edit your app and set the callback URL to
    https://groundwork.avijmahajan.com/api/zotero/callback.
  - Redeploy in Vercel (Deployments → latest production → ⋯ → Redeploy). Changed
    environment variables only apply after a redeploy.

  3. Redirect the old address
  - In Settings → Domains, edit lit-graph.avijmahajan.com and choose Redirect to
    groundwork.avijmahajan.com (permanent, 308).
  - This matters for this app: the server only accepts sign-ins on the address
    set in ZOTERO_OAUTH_ORIGIN. Without the redirect, anyone using the old link
    would hit a sign-in error.

  What users will notice: sign-in cookies belong to a single address, so
  everyone has to sign in with Zotero once on the new one. Nothing else is lost;
  their data lives in their own Zotero libraries.

  If you meant the *.vercel.app address: that comes from the project name.
  Change it under Settings → General → Project Name. It doesn't affect your
  custom domain.

  Once you've picked the address, I can update the README, which still mentions
  lit-graph.avijmahajan.com in the Zotero setup instructions.

✻ Brewed for 14s · done 7:37 PM

❯ is there similar software?                                                    

⏺ Web Search("AI tool check whether cited sources support claims in manuscript
            citation verification 2026")
  ⎿  Did 1 search in 5s

⏺ Web Search("citation graph literature mapping tool like Connected Papers
            Research Rabbit Litmaps 2026")
  ⎿  Did 1 search in 4s

⏺ Web Search("extract references from PDF import into Zotero tool")
  ⎿  Did 1 search in 6s

⏺ Yes. Each of Groundwork's three parts has well-known competitors, but I
  haven't found one product that combines them, especially with Zotero as the
  place your references and PDFs live. Grouped by tab:

  Explore papers (citation-graph exploration)
  - Connected Papers: builds a visual graph around one seed paper from Semantic
    Scholar data. Fast and simple, but the free tier allows only a few graphs a
    month. This is the closest match to the Explore tab.
  - ResearchRabbit: free citation and co-author mapping. Better at open-ended
    browsing than focused review.
  - Litmaps: citation maps on a timeline. Strong for tracking new papers that
    cite your set, and imports BibTeX/RIS.
  - Inciteful: free graphs from one or two seed papers. Undermind starts from a
    plain-language research question instead.

  Import references (bibliography → Zotero)
  - Zotero itself can't turn a PDF's reference list into library items. Its
    staff have said so on the forums.
  - Zotero plugins: Zotero-cita (extracts citations and shows which are already
    in your library), and a "Reference" add-on that lists a PDF's references in
    the reader.
  - Workarounds: run GROBID yourself, or paste the reference list into AnyStyle,
    export BibTeX, then import into Zotero.
  - None of these runs GROBID, matching, de-duplication and saving to Zotero as
    one flow, which is what the Import tab does.

  Check citations (do the sources support the claims?)
  - scite.ai: the best-known commercial tool in this area. It classifies how
    papers cite each other (supporting, contrasting or mentioning), and its
    Reference Check flags retracted or problematic references in a manuscript.
    But it judges citations across the published literature, not each claim in
    your draft against the PDF you cited.
  - Research prototypes from 2025–26 closest to the Check tab:
    - RefVerifier: checks reference metadata, finds open-access full texts,
      locates supporting passages and gives a verdict with an explanation.
    - SemanticCite and DeepSciVerify: full-text checks of whether a claim is
      supported. DeepSciVerify checks the abstract first and reads the full text
      only when it has to.
    - NVIDIA's citation-validation demo.
      These are mostly preprints, not finished products, and I haven't checked
      their accuracy claims.
  - Existence checkers such as CheckIfExist only confirm a reference is real.
    That catches made-up references, but not a real paper cited for something it
    doesn't say.

  What sets Groundwork apart
  - One workflow covering discovery, building your Zotero library, and checking
    claims, instead of three separate tools.
  - Claims are checked against your own Zotero PDFs, including paywalled ones
    you've added, with quotes and page numbers you can check yourself. The
    research tools mostly rely on open-access copies.
  - Your references stay in Zotero, which you already use, rather than in
    another tool's database.

  Where the others are ahead
  - Connected Papers and Litmaps have more polished graphs.
  - Litmaps alerts you to new papers that cite your set.
  - scite's database of how papers cite each other is far larger than anything
    Groundwork can build on the fly.

  All of this comes from a quick search today. I haven't used these tools
  myself, and pricing and features change often.

✻ Brewed for 29s · done 7:40 PM

❯ how do i increase it's google search visiblity                                

⏺ Right now Google has very little to work with. The site is a single page whose
  visible content is mostly the app itself (a search box, an empty graph,
  sign-in prompts), and there's no description, sitemap or robots.txt. Here's
  what would help, roughly in order of impact.

  1. Settle the domain first
  - Decide whether it's groundwork.avijmahajan.com or something else before
    doing any SEO work. The sitemap, canonical URL and Search Console all point
    at one address.
  - If you move from lit-graph…, the permanent redirect in Vercel (from my
    earlier steps) carries over whatever ranking the old address has.

  2. Give Google text to read: a real landing page
  This is the biggest lever. Search engines rank pages on their text, and the
  app has almost none. Two options:
  - Put a short public introduction above the app: what it does, the three
    steps, who it's for, and a screenshot.
  - Or move the app to /app and make / a proper landing page.

  Write it around what people actually search for, not the name. "Groundwork" is
  a common word, so you won't rank on the name alone. Phrases worth using:
  - "check if citations support claims";
  - "import references from a PDF into Zotero";
  - "citation graph explorer for Semantic Scholar".

  3. Technical basics (I can add these, about an hour of work)
  - A <meta name="description"> that becomes the snippet under your search
    result.
  - Open Graph / Twitter tags plus a share image, so links posted on Bluesky,
    Slack and so on show a proper preview card.
  - A canonical URL, robots.txt and sitemap.xml.
  - SoftwareApplication structured data (JSON-LD), which describes the app to
    Google.
  - One limitation: the tabs use #import-style links, and Google treats
    everything after # as the same page. If you want separate search results for
    each feature, they'd need real paths such as /import, each with its own
    text.

  4. Google Search Console
  - Verify your domain there (a DNS record, like the Vercel step), submit the
    sitemap, and request indexing.
  - It then shows which searches you appear for and flags any problems.
  - You can also make sure the Vercel preview addresses aren't indexed. Vercel
    normally marks previews as not-for-indexing, but Search Console is where
    you'd check.

  5. Links from other sites (this matters a lot for a new site)
  - University library guides: many maintain lists of literature-mapping and
    citation tools. The ones that came up in my last search (HKUST, UNB,
    PennWest, Monash Health) list Connected Papers, ResearchRabbit and Litmaps.
    Ask to be added.
  - The Zotero forums: people regularly ask how to pull a PDF's references into
    Zotero. A helpful answer that mentions the tool can bring in visitors long
    after it's posted.
  - The GitHub README, a short blog post or preprint describing how the citation
    check works, Product Hunt, and academic social media.

  A caveat: the useful features sit behind a Zotero sign-in limited to your
  allowlist. Visitors from Google won't be able to try them, so the landing page
  should say how to get access, such as a waitlist or a "request access" link.

  Do you want me to do step 3 now? Tell me the final domain, and whether you'd
  like the landing-page section added too.

  Created PR #8 (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ I added the landing page and the search-engine setup, all for
  groundwork.avijmahajan.com, and opened the PR:
  https://github.com/amahaja5/lit-graph/pull/8. All 159 tests pass. I checked
  the page at desktop and phone widths, and that old links still work.

  Landing page (/)
  - A headline ("Find the papers. Keep the references. Check the claims."), a
    short explanation, and an Open Groundwork button. Signed-in visitors see
    Continue instead.
  - How it works: three cards, one per tab, written in the words people search
    with: "citation graph", "extracts its bibliography", "save the cart to a
    Zotero collection", "supports, partly supports or contradicts".
  - Built around your Zotero library, What gets sent where, and a short FAQ.

  The app moves to /app. Old links like /#import forward automatically to
  /app#import, and clicking "Groundwork" in the app header goes back to the
  landing page. The app is hidden from Google, so search results point to the
  page that explains it.

  Search basics:
  - a search snippet description, a canonical URL, and a structured description
    of the app for Google;
  - a link preview card for social media and chat, using a new 1200×630 share
    image;
  - robots.txt and sitemap.xml;
  - unknown pages now return a real "not found" instead of quietly showing the
    app.

  Your steps
  1. Finish the domain move before merging. In Vercel, add
     groundwork.avijmahajan.com. Set ZOTERO_OAUTH_ORIGIN and the Zotero callback
     URL to the new address. Redirect the old domain to it. The canonical URL
     and sitemap already point at the new domain.
  2. After it's live: add the domain to Google Search Console, submit
     https://groundwork.avijmahajan.com/sitemap.xml, and request indexing. It
     usually takes days to a few weeks to appear in results.
  3. Getting access: the page says importing and checking need "an approved
     account", but there's no way to ask for one. If you want new users, add a
     request-access link or email address, and I'll put it on the page.

  Worth knowing: while checking that claim, I found that review generation 
  (/api/review/generate) works without signing in, and it runs on your Anthropic
  key. That was already the case before this PR, but a public landing page will
  bring more visitors. Want me to put it behind sign-in?

  Edited PR #8, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
⏺ Review generation now requires a Zotero sign-in, and the change is on PR #8
  (https://github.com/amahaja5/lit-graph/pull/8). I checked the PR was still
  open before pushing, and updated its description. All 161 tests pass.

  What changed:
  - Server: generating a review draft goes through the same check as import and
    audit: a signed-in, allowlisted Zotero account, and requests from your own
    site. It's checked on both the local Express server and the Vercel function,
    before Anthropic is called.
  - App: signed-out users who click Generate Review see "Could not generate
    review: Sign in with an allowed Zotero account to use this app."
  - Landing page: the access note now says generating review drafts needs a
    sign-in. Exploring papers, the graph, BibTeX and identifier exports stay
    open to everyone.

  Tests: a signed-out request gets 401 and never reaches Anthropic, and a
  signed-in request goes through. Both the Express server and the Vercel
  function are covered.

  One thing to know: in local development, with APP_AUTH_MODE unset, the old
  rule applies: requests from your own machine are allowed, and remote ones need
  AUDIT_ACCESS_TOKEN. Production runs with APP_AUTH_MODE=zotero, so this
  doesn't affect the live site.

  The domain steps from before still need doing before you merge #8: add the
  domain in Vercel, set ZOTERO_OAUTH_ORIGIN, update the Zotero callback URL, and
  redirect the old domain.

⏺ PR #8 is live on groundwork.avijmahajan.com, with the landing page, /app,

  Read 1 file, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ On Vercel, unknown paths like /nope still show the landing page with a 200,
  instead of a 404. That isn't coming from the repo: vercel.json now only
  rewrites /app, and the local server returns 404 as it should. Something in the
  Vercel project itself (probably a framework or fallback setting, or a cached
  response; the header says x-vercel-cache: HIT) is serving index.html. It's
  minor. The landing page's canonical tag points at /, so Google will treat
  those as duplicates of the homepage rather than penalise them. I can look into
  it next if you want.

  On your question: no. There's no waitlist or "request access" form on the page
  at all, so nothing emails you. The landing page only says importing, checking
  and review drafts need "a Zotero sign-in from an approved account". Visitors
  currently have no way to ask for access.

  Ways to add one, simplest first:

  1. Email link: a "Request access" button that opens the visitor's own email
     app, addressed to you. No server code and nothing to maintain. But some
     visitors won't have an email app set up, and the requests just arrive as
     normal emails.
  2. Hosted form (Tally, Google Forms or Formspree): a short form linked or
     embedded on the page. The service emails you each submission and keeps a
     list. Quick to set up, but it's another account to manage.
  3. Built in: a form on the landing page that posts to a new /api/waitlist
     endpoint, which emails you through a mail service such as Resend or
     Postmark. It looks the most polished and can collect the person's Zotero
     username, so you can add them to ZOTERO_ALLOWED_USERS directly. It needs
     that service's API key in Vercel, plus spam protection.

  Option 1 can be live in a few minutes, and option 3 takes longer. Which would
  you like, and which address should requests go to?

❯ 1 please                                                                      

⏺ I need one thing first: which email address should requests go to? It will be
  visible to anyone who views the page source, and spam bots scrape published
  addresses. So I'd suggest a dedicated alias, such as
  groundwork@avijmahajan.com if your domain's email allows it, rather than your
  main inbox. I'd also have the page assemble the address in JavaScript rather
  than write it in the HTML, which stops most simple scrapers.

  Here's what I'll build once you tell me the address:
  - A Request access button on the landing page: next to "Open Groundwork" in
    the hero, and in the access note.
  - Clicking it opens the visitor's email app with this filled in:
    - Subject: Groundwork access request
    - Body: prompts for their name, their Zotero username (what you add to
      ZOTERO_ALLOWED_USERS), and what they'd like to use it for.
  - A short note under the button showing the address, for visitors without an
    email app on that device.

  Which address should I use?

✻ Churned for 8s · done 8:00 PM

❯ there's already a saved email in vercel                                       

⏺ Good. That will be the CROSSREF_MAILTO address you added for Crossref, so I'll
  reuse it rather than hard-code anything. It works like this:
  - A tiny endpoint, GET /api/contact, returns the address from the server's
    environment. It uses a dedicated ACCESS_REQUEST_EMAIL if you ever set one,
    and otherwise falls back to CROSSREF_MAILTO.
  - The landing page fetches it and builds the Request access email link. The
    address never appears in the HTML, and if neither variable is set, the
    button simply doesn't appear.

  Created PR #9 (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ I added the Request access button, and it's in a PR:
  https://github.com/amahaja5/lit-graph/pull/9. All 163 tests pass, and I
  checked it in Chrome with a test address.

  - Where: next to Open Groundwork at the top of the landing page, and again at
    the bottom.
  - What it does: opens the visitor's email app with the subject "Groundwork
    access request" and a template asking for their name, their Zotero username
    (what you add to ZOTERO_ALLOWED_USERS), and what they'd like to use it for.
  - Fallback: a line under the button shows the address, for visitors whose
    device has no email app set up.
  - Address: it uses the email already saved in Vercel as CROSSREF_MAILTO, so
    there's nothing to configure. If you'd rather requests go elsewhere, set
    ACCESS_REQUEST_EMAIL in Vercel and it takes priority.
  - Spam: the address isn't written into the page's HTML. The page fetches it
    from the server, which keeps it away from simple scrapers.
  - Signed-in visitors don't see the button. If no valid address is set, it
    doesn't appear at all.

  Not checked yet: that it addresses the email you expect. I couldn't read your
  Vercel settings, so I only tested with a placeholder address. After merging,
  click Request access on groundwork.avijmahajan.com once to confirm it fills in
  the right address.

  Do you want me to merge it?

  the existing TypeSafe judgments integration can replace Claude for the claim
  checks. · summary

  Searched for 1 pattern, listed 1 directory, ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Here's where Claude is used today, what could replace each use, and one thing
  I need you to clarify.

  Where Claude (Anthropic) is used: five places

  #: 1
  Feature: Check citations: verdict (auditService.check)
  What Claude does: Reads the claim plus retrieved source passages; writes the
    verdict, explanation and quotes
  Possible replacement: A decision API: classify each claim–passage pair as
    supports / partly / contradicts / insufficient, with a confidence score
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Feature: Check citations: extraction (auditService.extract)
  What Claude does: Finds every citing sentence in the manuscript and maps it
    to its references
  Possible replacement: GROBID full-text mode: it already marks in-text
    citations (<ref type="bibr" target="#b12">) and links them to the
    bibliography, sentence by sentence. Your Cloud Run wrapper already
    supports mode=fulltext. No model needed.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Feature: Import: pasted text or Word files
  What Claude does: Pulls out the bibliography
  Possible replacement: GROBID's citation-list parser, though the wrapper
    doesn't expose it yet. Or accept PDFs only.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Feature: Explore: Generate Review
  What Claude does: Writes a structured literature synthesis
  Possible replacement: Nothing like-for-like. A decision API can't write
    prose, so this needs a local language model, or the feature has to go.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Feature: Review evidence ranking
  What Claude does: —
  Possible replacement: Already uses TypeSafe (optional), not Claude

  Why a decision API suits claim checking: the verdict becomes a label with a
  confidence score, and the "quote" is simply the passage that was judged, with
  its page number. So it can't invent a quote, which is the main risk with
  Claude here. Your TypeSafe experiments already point this way: the 16
  claim–source relation cases matched the expected labels 16/16, including
  tricky ones such as relative versus absolute numbers, hypotheses, and
  different populations. The catch is that you lose Claude's written
  explanations and suggested rewordings. You'd get a label, a confidence and the
  evidence passage instead.

  What I need you to clarify: what do you mean by a "local decisions API"?
  - (a) TypeSafe: the typed-judgment API already wired into the code
    (api.typesafe.ai, model jev-1.13.0). It's a hosted service, not local, but
    it's built for this kind of judgment, and your experiments put it at well
    under a cent per run.
  - (b) A model you run yourself: for example an open model served through
    Ollama or vLLM, or your own fine-tuned checkpoint. The import experiments
    already include a local-command adapter for that. Nothing leaves your
    infrastructure, but you'd need somewhere to host it (Cloud Run with a GPU,
    or your Akash GPU setup).
  - (c) Something else you have in mind. Tell me the name or URL.

  Also: should Generate Review be removed, or kept on a local language model?
  Under (a), it has to go.

  My suggestion: (a) for verdicts, GROBID full text for extraction, PDFs only
  for import, and remove Generate Review. That takes Anthropic out of the app
  entirely without new hosting.

      411 -## Optional TypeSafe evidence checks

      413 -TypeSafe can judge which HTML text is useful scholarly evidenc

      383 +Choose **Check citations** in the workspace navigation. Load a
          +n arXiv URL/identifier or upload the manuscript PDF (up to 3 M
          +B, including its bibliography). Two services do the work, both
          + called from the server:                                      
      384 
      415 -Set these server environment variables (locally in `.env` or t
          -he launching shell; on Vercel in project settings):           
      385 +1. **GROBID extracts the inventory.** The PDF goes to the GROB
          +ID service in full-text mode with sentence segmentation. Every
          + body sentence containing an in-text citation becomes a cited 
          +passage, linked to the bibliography entries its citation marke
          +rs point to (`<ref type="bibr" target="#b12">`). Bibliography 
          +entries keep their raw reference string, title, DOI and arXiv 
          +ID. Citations GROBID cannot link, and passages citing more tha
          +n eight sources, are reported as warnings. You can review and 
          +edit the inventory, including adding missed entries, before ch
          +ecking. Scanned PDFs need OCR first.                          
      386 +2. **TypeSafe judges each cited source.** For each source, the
          + app retrieves the PDF (from Zotero, or the public arXiv PDF f
          +or arXiv references), selects the most relevant passages (keyw
          +ord ranking, or TypeSafe ranking when `TYPESAFE_AUDIT_MODE=on`
          +), and asks TypeSafe how each of up to eight passages relates 
          +to the claim: `supported`, `partially_supported`, `contradicte
          +d` or `insufficient_evidence`, with a confidence. The rubric i
          +s the one evaluated in [the TypeSafe experiments](experiments/
          +typesafe/REPORT.md), where it matched 16/16 labelled claim–pas
          +sage cases.                                                   
      387 
      417 -```env                                                        
      418 -TYPESAFE_API_KEY=your_key                                     
      419 -TYPESAFE_MODEL=jev-1.13.0                                     
      420 -TYPESAFE_REVIEW_MODE=shadow                                   
      421 -TYPESAFE_AUDIT_MODE=shadow                                    
      422 -TYPESAFE_REVIEW_MIN_CONFIDENCE=0.9                            
      423 -```                                                           
      388 +Per-source verdicts follow the confident judgements (at or abo
          +ve `TYPESAFE_VERDICT_MIN_CONFIDENCE`, default 0.6): supporting
          + passages give **supported**, contradicting ones **contradicte
          +d**, both together or a narrower match **partially supported**
          +, and nothing confident **insufficient evidence** (the explana
          +tion notes which way the closest passage leaned). The evidence
          + shown for a verdict is the judged passage itself with its PDF
          + page, so quotes cannot be invented; `validateAssessment` stil
          +l requires every quote to be anchored to a retrieved page. The
          + combined finding is conservative: any unverified source keeps
          + the passage unverified, and conflicting sources cannot combin
          +e to "supported".                                             
      389 
      425 -Both modes default to `off`. `shadow` runs judgments and recor
          -ds diagnostics while retaining the existing selected evidence;
          - `on` applies the judgments. Use `on` for an explicit pilot af
          -ter reviewing shadow results. Restart the server after changin
          -g configuration. The 0.9 HTML confidence threshold is a provis
          -ional conservative default, **not calibrated on a representati
          -ve manuscript corpus**. Small synthetic experiments motivated 
          -this integration; real-document evaluation remains necessary b
          -efore broad rollout.                                          
      390 +These are **automated support judgements**, not certification 
          +of printed pagination or experimental validity. They come with
          + a confidence and the passage they rest on; read the passage b
          +efore relying on a verdict. Retrieval selects excerpts rather 
          +than every page, so missing evidence stays unverified. Small s
          +ynthetic experiments motivated the rubric and threshold; calib
          +rate on real manuscripts before relying on them.              
      391 
      427 -In review `on` mode, a standard HTML parser retains multiple c
          -ontent regions and short passages, then TypeSafe selects usefu
          -l text blocks. Rejected or uncertain blocks are omitted. Missi
          -ng credentials or service failure falls back to available abst
          -racts with a warning. In audit `on` mode, TypeSafe scores supp
          -orting and contradicting evidence alike; failure uses the orig
          -inal keyword selector with a warning. Audit source matching, q
          -uotation validation, and combined-source assessment are unchan
          -ged in responsibility.                                        
      392 +The audit runs one passage at a time. Stop retains completed f
          +indings; Resume retries failed/unprocessed passages. Download 
          +Markdown or JSON, including pending/failed entries and TypeSaf
          +e model, rubric and selection diagnostics. Changing the manusc
          +ript invalidates the inventory; changing a source or collectio
          +n invalidates results. State lives in memory and is lost on re
          +fresh. The manuscript PDF goes to GROBID and claims with selec
          +ted source passages go to TypeSafe only when the user starts e
          +xtraction or checking; originals are not modified or written i
          +nto Zotero.                                                   
      393 
      429 -Requests use a pinned model, typed response validation, bounde
          -d retries, at most two concurrent calls per shared client, and
          - a ten-second TypeSafe budget starting at the first judgment i
          -n each operation. API judgments are cached in memory for five 
          -minutes, up to 128 entries. HTML fetches are capped at 2 MB; c
          -andidate extraction at 80 blocks / 80,000 characters. Large PD
          -Fs use at most 96 candidates combining lexical matches and pag
          -e coverage, with a coverage warning; this shortlist does not g
          -uarantee complete retrieval. Model/rubric/configuration change
          -s invalidate relevant cache keys. Transient HTML failures are 
          -not cached as successful evidence or reviews.                 
      430 -                                                              
      431 -Enabling either `shadow` or `on` sends candidate source text a
          -nd relevant paper metadata/claims to TypeSafe. Keys stay on th
          -e server. Review reference `quality` and audit source `selecti

      437 -Choose **Check citations** in the workspace navigation. Load a
          -n arXiv URL/identifier, upload a PDF, DOCX, TXT, or Markdown f
          -ile (up to 3 MB), or paste up to 100,000 characters including 
          -the bibliography. The app extracts citation-bearing passages a
          -nd reference mappings, lets you review/edit source titles, ide
          -ntifiers, passages, and citation mappings (including adding mi
          -ssed entries), and checks the cited claims against retrieved P
          -DFs. A passage can cite up to eight sources. Larger jobs must 
          -be divided into sections with their bibliography (80 reference
          -s / 150 passages per inventory).                              
      438 -                                                              
      439 -Each passage receives per-source findings and a combined asses
          -sment: supported, partially supported, contradicted, or insuff
          -icient evidence. Positive/negative support findings require an
          - exact quoted passage anchored to a retrieved PDF page. The re
          -port preserves source SHA-256, retrieval route, version, Zoter
          -o keys, and reviewed PDF page indices. These are **AI-assisted
          - support assessments**, not a certification of printed paginat
          -ion or experimental validity. Retrieval selects relevant excer
          -pts rather than assessing every page; missing evidence stays u
          -nverified. Inventory extraction may miss citations and should 
          -be reviewed. Source PDFs without readable text need OCR outsid
          -e this web workflow.                                          
      440 -

          -indings; Resume retries failed/unprocessed passages. Download 
          -Markdown or JSON, including pending/failed entries. Editing ma
          -nuscript text invalidates the inventory; changing a source or 
          -collection invalidates results. State lives in memory and is l
          -ost on refresh. Uploaded manuscripts and selected source excer
          -pts are sent to Anthropic only when the user initiates extract
          -ion/checking; originals are not modified or written into Zoter
          -o. The web workflow exports findings and suggested revisions, 
          -not an automatically rewritten manuscript.

      1 ## Summary
      2 Groundwork no longer uses Anthropic. Citation **extraction** runs

      4 **Extraction (GROBID full text)**
